Both go through one queue on a worker (`ScriptCommandsViewModel.Enqueue`, `WhenHooksDoneAsync` for tests); a `ReaderResized` that waits is replaced by the newer size. `Startup` runs once when the window is open and a library is open (`WindowShown`, `OnHostOpened`); `Shutdown` runs in `ShutdownRequested` (a script can refuse a user close) or at exit; `StoreCommandStates` runs before the preferences are flushed.
- Menus: File > Automation (`Library`), the `NewBooks` items behind a line after the import item, the context menu's `miAutomation` (`Books`), the toolbar buttons of the `Books` commands that have a picture (`CrToolButton`, or `CrSplitButton` with Configure... when `Command.Configure` is set; they sit before the quick search), the split button of the Info dialog (`Editor`, `BookInfoRequest.EditorScripts/RunEditorScript`, `BookEditorModel.RunScriptAsync`), Window > Script Output (mac-only). A picture of a script is a file next to the script: `Icons.RegisterFile` gives it a name (`script:<key>:<file>`) so views only name pictures. The engine gives the first twelve enabled commands Ctrl+Shift+F1 to F12; `ScriptCommandItem.Gesture` maps them to Cmd+Shift+F1 to F12 (the native File menu items carry them).
- **The `Editor` hook has no delegate type in `PythonCommand`**: a command that says only `#@Hook Editor` is never called; the original's scripts say `#@Hook Books, Editor`. Such a command is also a Books command.
- Bundled scripts that load on the Mac today (without P4.8): `CommitProposed.py`, `OtherScripts.py`, `Autonumber.py`, `RefreshView.py`, `NewComics.py`, `SearchAndReplace.py` give commands (the e2e log says `Scripting: 25 commands`): `clr.AddReferenceByPartialName("System.Windows.Forms")` does not fail the load, only the use of a type from it does. So the `Books`, `Library` and `NewBooks` lists of a test that starts the real host contain these commands; tests look their own commands up by key.
- The script output is `ScriptConsoleLog` (a `TextWriter`, at most 1,000,000 characters, the oldest text goes first), attached with `AttachConsole(log, log: true)` (so `Calling 'X'...` and `Compilation of ...` lines are in it, as the original's console has them). `PythonCommand.Output` is process wide: one host at a time, as before. The window (`ScriptConsoleWindow`, 606 x 471) follows the text with a 100 ms timer; it opens with `--show-script-console` (env `COMICRACK_MAC_SCRIPT_CONSOLE=1`, the original's `-ssc`) or Window > Script Output, takes the menu bar of the program (`NativeMenu.SetMenu` per window, otherwise File and the others disappear while it has the focus) and keeps its frame in `AppPreferences.ScriptOutputWindow`. Contact sheet name: `--show ScriptOutputForm`.
- `AppScriptUi`: `Restart` and `ScanFolders` are only logged until P5.10b and P5.19b; `EditNewBook` uses the Info dialog on the new book (P5.20 has the fileless book dialog); `MainWindow` (the `IWin32Window` a script gets) is null until the script UI layer exists; `ComicRack.ComicDisplay` maps `PageLayout`, `ImageFitMode`, `ImageZoom` and `RightToLeftReading` to the current reader and keeps the other display settings.
- Tests: Host.Tests `ScriptCommandRunnerTests` (5: worker thread and output, error unwrapping, cancel with a running script, a script that never prints and a run cancelled before it starts, the log), Mac.Tests `Scripting/ScriptCommandsTests` (15: lists, run with undo marker, progress and Cancel, error report, questions 0/1/2, `UiCall`, Startup once, Shutdown veto, BookOpened and ReaderResized, `ComicDisplay`, Info dialog script button, menus and context menu, toolbar button and output window, picture registry), e2e case `scripts-menu` (a generic test script in `<data>/Data/Scripts`: File > Automation runs a command on two books, the output window, Window > Script Output, Startup, BookOpened and ReaderResized through a file the script writes, the saved library).
**Facts from P4.7 (2026-10-27)**
- `mac/tools/facade-gen` (`FacadeGen.csproj`, in the solution): `dotnet run --project mac/tools/facade-gen [--check] [--reference-pack DIR] [--root DIR] [--forms-out DIR] [--drawing-out DIR]`. Exit 0 ok, 1 `--check` found a difference, 2 bad arguments, 3 no reference pack (run a pass A build), 4 findings in the hand-written sources. It reads `System.Windows.Forms` and `System.Windows.Forms.Primitives` (to `mac/cYo.Compat.Forms/Generated`) and `System.Drawing.Common` (to `mac/cYo.Compat.Drawing/Generated`) of the Windows Desktop pack with a `MetadataLoadContext` and the hand-written shim sources with Roslyn (syntax only). Writes only changed files and removes stale ones; two runs give the same files.
- **Scope = the whole surface, not only Tier A and B**: every visible type, nested ones included (996 top-level and 281 nested types, about 11,900 members, 964 `.g.cs` files, 52,000 lines, 3 MB), so a Tier C type gives the standard `NotSupportedException` instead of an `AttributeError`. The pack is not a true reference assembly: it holds the internal members and types too (1791 types in `System.Windows.Forms`), so the generator keeps only public and protected ones. Output: `Generated/<namespace>/<Type>.g.cs` (partial declarations, `global::` names, tabs), `FacadeStub.g.cs` (internal `FacadeStub.Member(name)` builds the exception: message `<Type.Member> is not yet supported on macOS (ComicRack script compatibility layer)`, constructors say `.ctor`), `omitted.txt` (what is left out: the Win32 struct `System.Drawing.Interop.LOGFONT` and `Font.FromLogFont/ToLogFont`; the interfaces `Accessibility.IAccessible` of `AccessibleObject` and an `IComparer<internal type>` of `ComboBox.ObjectCollection`; the internal CsWin32 interfaces that the pack lists on its classes are dropped silently). The facade assemblies are about 1.7 MB (Forms) and 0.26 MB (Drawing); a clean build of the solution is unchanged in seconds.
- **How the hand-written part merges** (rule for P4.9 to P4.13): the shim's own file of a type is `partial` (12 Forms and 25 Drawing types were made partial; `Form` now derives from `ContainerControl` as in the reference, `DataObject` names the COM interface `System.Runtime.InteropServices.ComTypes.IDataObject` in full because `System.Windows.Forms.IDataObject` now exists, `DataFormats.Format`'s constructor, `Brush()` and a few write-only properties got the reference's accessibility and accessors). To implement a member: declare it in that file with the reference's signature, accessibility, modifiers and parameter names, run the generator, commit both; the generated declaration disappears (until then the compiler says CS0111). Members are matched by name and simple parameter types (`MemberKeys`); a hand-written type without `partial` is left alone only when it lacks nothing (else exit 4), a differing base class is a finding, base class and interfaces that the hand-written part leaves out are added by the generated part, a hand-written class with no constructor keeps its implicit one (the generated part declares an empty parameterless constructor with the reference's accessibility, because another constructor would remove the implicit one). Enums and delegates written by hand are never generated.
- **Bodies**: every body throws, with exceptions that must not: `Dispose()`/`Dispose(bool)` do nothing (an override calls the base), `ToString`, `GetHashCode` and `Equals(object)` overrides call the base, `==` and `!=` of a type with itself compare references (classes) or call `Equals(object)` (structs), `Finalize` is not declared. A `Component` finalizer calls the virtual `Dispose(false)`: a throwing override ended the process on the finalizer thread after every failed constructor (`Objects_whose_constructor_threw_are_finalized_without_crashing_the_process`). Events throw on `+=` and `-=` (a script learns at once that the event never fires). Constructors throw after the base call; when the base has no parameterless constructor the call is `: base(default(T1), ...)` with the fewest parameters; a class whose reference has no accessible constructor gets `internal T()`. Consequence: `new Form()` throws from `ContainerControl..ctor` now (Form was a bare Control before) until P4.9a writes the chain. `static readonly` fields of generated types are `default` (null/zero: `DataFormats.Text` and the other format names, `DataGridView.HitTestInfo.Nowhere`, `TaskDialogIcon.*`, `Padding.Empty` is hand-written); constants keep their values; a type that implements something must give them real values.
- **Drift guards**: `Compat.Forms.Tests` and `Compat.Drawing.Tests` `ShimSurfaceTests` (shared logic in `mac/tests/Shared/SurfaceComparison.cs`, linked into both) compare every visible reference type with the facade in both directions: kind, base type, interfaces, abstract/sealed, enum values and `[Flags]`, and each public/protected member with accessibility, static/abstract/virtual/override, parameter names, `params`, defaults and constants (a hand-written protected override that the reference lacks is allowed; `virtual final newslot` counts as plain, it depends on the internal interfaces). `FacadeGeneratorTests`: the committed `Generated` folders equal the generator's output now (regenerate when it fails: also after a newer SDK brings a newer pack, here 10.0.13), the merge rules above on synthetic hand-written sources, `MemberKeys` agree between syntax and metadata. `GeneratedStubTests` (both projects): messages, hand-written parts keep working, finalization. Host.Tests `FacadeCatalogTests`: `ApiCatalog.FromFacade` (now `IsComplete`, reads `cYo.Compat.*` before the `System.*` names because the output folder also holds the real `System.Drawing.Common` 4.7.0 of IronPython) has the types and members of `FromReferenceDirectory`, and `ApiTiers.Listed()` names only types of the surface (two dead names were fixed: `ListViewSubItem`, `HtmlElementEventHandler`; `compat-usage.md` did not change); `GeneratedSurfaceScriptTests` runs a Python script over the surface, including `class UIControl(Control)` with `OnPaint` and `OnResize` overrides on the generated `Control`.
- **IronPython**: a .NET exception shows in Python as `SystemError`; read `e.message`, not `e.Message`. With the complete overload set a constant can bind to another overload than before: `EncoderParameter(Encoder, 85)` binds `(Encoder, byte)` as on Windows; the Core passes variables, so it still binds the hand-written `(Encoder, long)` (pass B census 0 errors); the tests use `85L`. `new ColorMatrix(null)` is ambiguous as in System.Drawing. The DPI members (`Graphics.DpiX`, `Image.HorizontalResolution`, `SetResolution`, `PageScale`) now exist and throw; `DrawingHiDpiTests` pins that.
- The catalog reader `ApiCatalog.IsPublic` counts public nested types of non-public types (`IExtender+Vtbl`, `PrintingServices+DOCINFO`) and omits protected nested ones, so "1111 types" of `compat-usage.md` is not the 996 + 281 of the reference (section 9).

**Facts from P4.8 (2026-10-27)**
- The identity of S6 is applied by project files only: `AssemblyName` `System.Windows.Forms` (`mac/cYo.Compat.Forms`) and `System.Drawing.Common` (`mac/cYo.Compat.Drawing`), both `AssemblyVersion`/`FileVersion` 10.0.0.0 and unsigned; `ComicRack.Engine`, `ComicRack.Plugins`, `cYo.Common` for the three shadow projects (project and folder names, namespaces and `RootNamespace` are unchanged; pass A builds the same names). The real `System.Drawing.Common` 4.7.0 that IronPython depends on needs no `ExcludeAssets`: NuGet resolves the package id to the project of the same name, so every output folder (and the bundle) holds exactly one `System.Drawing.Common.dll`, the facade (the assets file shows it as `System.Drawing.Common.Reference`); `AssemblyIdentityTests` pins it, and the absence of the old file names (stale `cYo.Compat.*.dll`, `*.Core.dll` of an older build in a `bin` folder are what to delete after pulling this commit).
- New project `mac/cYo.Common.Windows.Compat` (in the solution under `/shims/`, referenced by `ComicRack.Mac.Host`): `AssemblyName` `cYo.Common.Windows` 1.0.0.0, namespace `cYo.Common.Windows.Forms`. It declares, with the signatures of the original, the members of `FormUtility` that need no WinForms handle: `FindActiveService` (static, and the `Control` extension), `InvokeActiveService`, `FindServices`, `FindFirstService`, `FindParentService`, `ServiceTranslation`, `DpiScale` (always 1,1) and the `ScaleDpi*` family; the rest of `FormUtility` (panels and list views filled from options, form positions, tool strip helpers, `Bitmap.ScaleDpi`) and every other type of `cYo.Common.Windows` are added by the tasks that implement their controls (P4.9 onward, P4.19 lists the gaps). The static `FindActiveService<T>()` asks the program first: `ActiveServices.Resolver` (set by `ScriptHost.Start` from `IScriptUi.FindActiveService(Type)`, removed by `Dispose`), then walks the control tree of `Form.ActiveForm` as the original. `AppScriptUi` answers `IRefreshDisplay` (the reader shows its page again, the browser redraws its covers); `HeadlessScriptUi.Services` lets a test provide services. `RefreshView.py` runs unmodified (test `RefreshView_py_reaches_the_refresh_service...`).
- `clr.AddReference('System.Drawing')` and `AddReferenceByPartialName('System.Drawing')` land on the facade through the framework's own `System.Drawing.dll` forwarders (`Bitmap`, `Graphics`, `Font` report assembly `System.Drawing.Common 10.0.0.0`); `Color`, `Point`, `Size`, `Rectangle` stay the framework's `System.Drawing.Primitives` types, which the facade does not redeclare. The strong-name spellings of the .NET Framework assemblies (`System.Windows.Forms, Version=4.0.0.0, PublicKeyToken=b77a5c561934e089`, `System.Drawing, Version=4.0.0.0, PublicKeyToken=b03f5f7f11d50a3a`) bind to the facades. No resolver hook is installed anywhere.
- The unmodified scripts of `ComicRack/Output/Scripts` (7 `.py`) load: `AssemblyIdentityTests.Every_unmodified_bundled_script_loads_without_a_reference_error` (byte-identical copy, `PreCompile` of the first command runs the top level), `BundledSampleTests` run the Sample hooks, `CommitProposed` and `SetComicInfoDirty` from that copy (`ScriptTestSupport.RewrittenCopyOfBundledScripts` is gone). The UI hooks of `Sample.py` and `Autonumber.py` still stop at `ContainerControl..ctor` (P4.9a).
- Bundle: `mac/tools/make-app.sh` fails when one of the six assemblies is missing from `Contents/MacOS` or one of the five old names is there; `mac/tools/bundle-selftest.sh [App.app]` runs `mac/scripting/identity-probe.py` and the unmodified `Contents/MacOS/Scripts/Sample.py` through the bundle's own `--script-selftest` (33 PASS, 0 FAIL, hardened runtime, ad-hoc signature). `ScriptSelfTestTests.The_identity_probe_and_the_unmodified_Sample_py_pass_...` runs the same file in the test process. `COMICRACK_SELFTEST_REWRITE_REFS`, `COMICRACK_SELFTEST_RESOLVER` and the S4 variants `patched-rewrite`/`patched-resolver` are removed; `mac/spikes/S4-ironpython/selftest.py` lost its three scenarios that named `cYo.Compat.*` and uses the new names (`run.sh patched`: all checks pass); the S6 `identity.sh core` is historical (it renamed outputs that are now built under the final names).
- Tools and tests that followed the rename: `api-inventory` (`ShimCoverage` looks for `System.Windows.Forms.dll`/`System.Drawing.Common.dll` in the shim project folders, `Assemblies` lists the three Core names), `NativeLibraryResolver.CoreAssemblies` (comments), `WindowsTripwireTests`, `ApiCatalog.FromFacade` (reads the two `System.*` names from the output folder), and the shared drift guard `mac/tests/Shared/SurfaceComparison.cs`: the facade and the pack's reference assembly now have the same identity (name and version), so `SurfaceFixture` uses two `MetadataLoadContext`s (`Context` for the reference, `FacadeContext` for the facades and SkiaSharp) and compares by names; the unused `ReferencePack.CreateContext` of both test projects is gone. A new test that loads a facade and a pack assembly of one name into one context fails with a file-load error.
- `mac/reports/api-inventory.md` and `census*.md` still print the old project/assembly names; they are regenerated by the tools and carry no figure that changed.

**Facts from P4.17 (2026-10-27)**
- Tools: `crcli plugin-gap <package.crplugin>... [--rules F] [--notes-dir D] [--markdown F] [--json F] [--reference-pack D] [--require-tasks]` and `mac/scripting/plugin-gap.sh [--check]` (writes `mac/reports/plugin-gap-bedetheque.md`, committed, no times and no machine paths, same text on every run; JSON with every row in `mac/artifacts/plugin-gap/gap.json`, git ignored; exit 1 when a row that needs work names no task). Code in `mac/ComicRack.Mac.Host/Scripting/Scan/`: `PluginGapRunner` (whole analysis), `PluginProbe` (install through `PluginPackages.InstallNow` into a throw-away data folder, command list, load check: the top level of each script with commands runs through `PythonCommand.PreCompile`; this is the only place the code of a package runs), `PluginGapBuilder`/`PluginGap` (the tables), `TypeIndex` (public types and members of the assemblies of the program and the runtime read from metadata), `GapRules`, `GapReportWriter`, `PythonModuleCatalog` (built-in and `Lib` modules). Planning data: `mac/scripting/gap-rules.txt` (`owner <pattern> <task>`, `note <pattern> <differs|-> <text>`, first match wins, `owner finding:<kind> <task>` for scanner findings) and hand-written sections `mac/scripting/gap-notes/<package file name without extension>.md`. Tests: Host.Tests `Scripting/PluginGapTests` (15), `Scripting/JavaScriptSerializerTests` (17), `Acceptance/PluginGapAcceptanceTests` (6, skipped when the packages are not in `mac/fixtures/plugins`), Cli.Tests `PluginNamesRuleTests`.
- Status of a row (closed set, one per row): `real`, `stub`, `partial`, `missing`, `differs`, `plugin`, `python`, `dynamic`. **Stub rule of the index**: a method of a facade is a stub when its IL ends in `call FacadeStub.Member(string); throw` (the generated body), a static read-only field of a facade type without static constructor keeps its default value and counts as a stub, a property or event takes the status of its accessors, overloads give `partial` when they differ. The status of a facade member therefore follows the hand-written partial types by itself: re-run `plugin-gap.sh` after a task changes a facade (the report is a snapshot of 2026-10-27; there is no drift test on purpose, P4.19 regenerates it). `differs` comes only from `note` lines of the rules (measured facts about .NET 10 members); `plugin` = a name the script defines itself, `python` = a member of a Python built-in type, `dynamic` = no known type has a member of that name. A by-name row (an access on a value of unknown type) is classified by the first group that has a member of that name: Python built-ins, the plugin's own names, the types the plugin uses (WinForms, .NET, Core), all Core types, the imported .NET namespaces, the WinForms/GDI+ surface; `partial` there means the candidates differ, so it is a candidate list and not proof of use.
- What a scan records now (generic, not in the JSON of `script-scan`): `ScanUnit.Foreign` (dotted names of .NET types and members outside the surface, including names bound by `from <Core namespace> import *` when `ScriptScanner.ForeignTypeLookup` is set, and member references of assemblies), `Modules` (Python imports), `Defined` (functions, classes, class attributes and `self.x` the script defines); bundled copies of standard library modules in a package (`os.py`, `urllib2.py` ...) are left out of the module table and of the plugin's own names. Scanner corrections made on the way (re-run `mac/scripting/scan-corpus.sh` for `compat-usage.md`): a name after a star import binds to a catalog type of any star import before a Windows-only guess (after `from System.Windows.Forms import *` and `from System.Drawing import *`, `Bitmap` is the drawing type and `ComicRack` stays unknown instead of becoming `System.Windows.Forms.ComicRack`); `System.Web.Extensions` is a `framework` assembly (the layer provides it); a string such as `"\App.Config"` (backslash plus file name, as joined to a folder) is a `windows-path` warning.
- Provided here: `mac/cYo.Compat.Web` (assembly `System.Web.Extensions` 10.0.0.0, namespace `System.Web.Script.Serialization`: `JavaScriptSerializer`, `JavaScriptConverter`, `JavaScriptTypeResolver`, `SimpleTypeResolver`, `ScriptIgnoreAttribute`) with the Framework's behaviour (escaping of `'`, `<`, `>`; `\/Date(ms)\/`; enums as numbers; fields then properties; numbers parse to int, long, decimal, double; arrays are `ArrayList` in a typed graph and `object[]` from `DeserializeObject`; `MaxJsonLength` 2,097,152 and `RecursionLimit` 100 by default). It is a reference of the Host project, so every output folder and the bundle have it (`make-app.sh` checks it; the identity probe has a scenario: `mac/tools/bundle-selftest.sh` reports 36 PASS, 0 FAIL in the signed bundle). A dictionary that is also a sequence of its keys (a Python dict) is written through its `IDictionaryEnumerator`.
- Results of the first run: Bedetheque Scraper 2 6.08: tier A, 32 Python files and the native `BedethequeFetcher.exe`, 528 rows (194 real, 110 stub, 12 partial, 1 differs, 7 plugin, 73 python, 131 dynamic), top level runs; Library Organizer 2.1.13: tier C, 11 Python files, 642 rows (211 real, 248 stub, 18 partial, 11 missing, 1 differs), the top level fails with "Could not add reference to assembly PresentationFramework". Every stub or missing row belongs to P3.4, P3.5a, P4.9a to P4.9f, P4.10a to P4.10g, P4.13a to P4.13c, P4.19, P4.20 or one of P4.17.1 to P4.17.6 (the Task column).
- **Bedetheque helper** (details in the report): the plugin starts `BedethequeFetcher.exe` (a native Windows PyInstaller program in the package) as `Process` with `--parent_pid <host pid>`, probes `127.0.0.1:56789` with `TcpClient`, waits up to 20 s, and speaks one JSON line each way over a new TCP connection per page (`{"url": ...}` then `{"url": ..., "html": ...}` or `{"error": ...}`; JSON through `JavaScriptSerializer`); `use_cloudscraper` starts the helper per page with `--cloudscraper --url` and reads standard output. `File.Exists(exe)` is tested before the fallback `python BedethequeFetcher.py` (not in the package; `python` is not installed on a stock Mac, only `python3`), so on macOS the exe is always chosen and `Process.Start` fails with "Exec format error" (`WindowsError 22`, measured). The protocol itself needs nothing from the layer (loopback TCP, `Process`, threads are .NET 10).
- **Blockers the report found that the P4.6 scan could not see**: (1) both plugins join file names with a backslash (`path + "\BDTranslations.Config"`): on macOS `File.Exists` is false and `Translate()` of the first plugin ends the script with `sys.exit(0)` before any dialog (measured: every hook ended with `SystemExit`), the second cannot load its toolbar images (P4.17.5); (2) the Library Organizer imports WPF at the top level (`PresentationFramework`, `PresentationCore`, `XamlReader`, `BitmapImage`, `WindowInteropHelper`) so it does not load at all, although the XAML dialog is only built when a move finds a duplicate (P4.17.3 for the names, P4.17.6 for the dialog); (3) `Microsoft.VisualBasic.FileIO.FileSystem.DeleteFile(path, OnlyErrorDialogs, SendToRecycleBin)` throws "UI not available for copy or move" (measured), the plugin then reports the delete as failed and does not move the file (P4.17.4); (4) `System.Diagnostics` types need `clr.AddReference('System')` in IronPython on .NET 10 (the plugins do it; a bare `from System.Diagnostics import Process` raises "Cannot import name").
- Corpus records: the Library Organizer package (126,112 bytes, SHA-256 `9ef9617d349fc212396409d84a3c3b2c927105257bb18f5fc63cabd284312320`) was fetched by `fetch-corpus.sh` into `mac/fixtures/plugins/library-organizer-2.1.13/`; GitHub reports no licence, the file headers say Apache-2.0 (`fetch-corpus.sh`, `corpus.md` and its `SOURCE.txt` say so now). The Bedetheque Scraper 2 repository states no licence (the file stays local). Neither package is committed or run outside the plugin host.
- `mac/tools/plan-status/plan_status.py` accepts task ids with a third part (`P4.17.1`): the wave order, the dependency list and the run log use it. The old test `The_program_code_names_no_single_plugin` of `CorpusToolTests` is replaced by `PluginNamesRuleTests` (tools, spikes and tests are no longer exempt; only `Acceptance` tests, the plan, reports, scripting data and fixtures may name a plugin).

**Facts from P4.17.3 (2026-10-27)**
- Generator (`mac/tools/facade-gen`): `ReferenceSurface.WpfFacades` lists the facade assemblies and the reference assemblies each declares (`System.Xaml`; `PresentationCore` = `WindowsBase` + `PresentationCore`; `PresentationFramework`); output `mac/cYo.Compat.Xaml`, `mac/cYo.Compat.PresentationCore`, `mac/cYo.Compat.PresentationFramework` (`Generated/`, `omitted.txt`, `Forwards.g.cs`); `--wpf-out DIR`, `GeneratorOptions.WpfOutputRoot`, `GeneratorResult.Wpf`. Forms and Drawing output is byte-identical to before. New generator rules (they apply to every set): a member that names a type outside the facades and the runtime (`System.Printing`, `UIAutomationProvider`, `ReachFramework`, 107 members, listed in `omitted.txt`) is left out; a class whose every constructor is left out gets the internal constructor, not the implicit public one; `[In] ref T` of a COM interface is a `ref` parameter (the keyword `in` needs the compiler's `IsReadOnly` marker); a file that uses a pointer type gets `unsafe`; type forwards of the reference assemblies to the runtime or to another facade become `[assembly: TypeForwardedTo]` (`Forwards.g.cs`), forwards to assemblies we do not have (`System.Windows.Extensions`, `System.Security.Permissions`) are listed in `omitted.txt`; the facades see each other's internals (`InternalsVisibleTo`), because a derived class calls the internal constructors of its base.
- **Name clash with the runtime's `WindowsBase.dll`**: the deps entry of a project reference carries no assembly version, so the host treats an app-local `WindowsBase.dll` as 0.0.0.0 and the runtime's 4.0.0.0 wins (a higher `AssemblyVersion`, a `PackageId` that sorts later and `IncludeRuntimeFileVersions` were tried; the `COREHOST_TRACE` log shows the TPA choice). The `WindowsBase` types are therefore declared in the `PresentationCore` facade. Any other assembly name that the runtime ships (P4.17.4: `Microsoft.VisualBasic`) has the same problem.
- **Compile visibility**: `System.Windows.Input.Key`/`KeyBinding` clash with Avalonia's, so no project may compile against the WPF facades. `mac/Directory.Build.targets` (`HideWpfFacadesFromCompile`) removes the three assemblies from the compile references of every project whose name does not start with `cYo.Compat.` (property `WpfFacadesVisible=true` opts in); `ComicRack.Mac.Host` references the three projects, so the files flow to every output folder, the tests and the bundle (`make-app.sh` checks them). `Compat.Forms.Tests` reads them with a `MetadataLoadContext`.
- **IronPython and `System.dll`**: IronPython on the Framework starts with `System.dll` loaded, on .NET 10 `from System import Uri` fails (types live in `System.Private.Uri`, `System.Diagnostics.Process` ...) until `clr.AddReference("System")`; `site`/`sitecustomize` are not imported by the hosting API. Fix: pass B patched copy of `ComicRack.Plugins/PythonCommand.cs` (`mac/ComicRack.Plugins.Core/Replacements/PassB/PythonCommand.cs`, manifest entry, 3 changed lines) calls `ScriptEngineDefaults.Prepare` (`mac/ComicRack.Plugins.Core/Compat/ScriptEngineDefaults.cs`), which does `engine.Runtime.LoadAssembly(Assembly.Load("System"))`. `from System.Diagnostics import Process` now works without a reference line too.
- Tests: `Compat.Forms.Tests/WpfSurfaceTests` (identity of the three files; every type, shape and member of the four reference assemblies against the facades, `SurfaceFixture` got `packAssemblies`, which also switches on "a type outside the facades counts as omitted"), `FacadeGeneratorTests` (committed `Generated` folders, determinism, WPF included), Host.Tests `AssemblyIdentityTests` (script reaches the names; message of a stub; files in the output folder), `identity-probe.py` (two scenarios, 47 checks in the signed bundle), `PluginGapAcceptanceTests` (the Library Organizer loads). The gap report is regenerated: WPF rows are `stub` with owner P4.17.6 (`gap-rules.txt`), the assemblies are `real`, no `missing` row is left for the Library Organizer.


**Facts from P4.17.4 (2026-10-28)**
- **An app-local assembly wins over a framework file of the same name only with a version in the deps entry** (generalises the P4.17.3 WindowsBase fact): for a project reference the SDK writes `"Name.dll": { "fileVersion": "0.0.0.0" }` without `assemblyVersion`, the host reads that entry first and replaces it by the framework's (`COREHOST_TRACE` line "Replacing deps entry"). `mac/Directory.Build.targets` target `StampVisualBasicVersionBuild`/`...Publish` (task `StampDepsVersion`, idempotent regex edit) writes `assemblyVersion` and `fileVersion` 10.0.1.0 for `Microsoft.VisualBasic.dll` into every deps file after it is generated. `LoadFromAssemblyPath`/`LoadFromStream` into the default context fail with `FileLoadException` for a name in the framework's list. The same stamp would make a WindowsBase facade of a version above 4.0.0.0 possible (not needed now).
- `Microsoft.VisualBasic.dll` of the runtime has no type, it forwards to `Microsoft.VisualBasic.Core` 15.0.0.0 (where `FileSystem` and the enums `UIOption`, `RecycleOption`, `UICancelOption`, `DeleteDirectoryOption` live). The UI-free members of the framework's `FileSystem` work on macOS; only the `UIOption` overloads throw. The new assembly reaches the framework's type under the extern alias `vbcore` (`AliasVisualBasicCore` target in the project; the test project has the same target). A project that compiles against it and also needs the framework's `FileSystem` must alias `Microsoft.VisualBasic.Core` the same way (both have the full name `Microsoft.VisualBasic.FileIO.FileSystem`).
- Services: `ComicRack.Compat.VisualBasic.FileSystemServices.Trash` (`ITrash`) and `.Ui` (`IFileSystemUi`), process-wide, set by `ScriptHost` (`VisualBasicRuntime.Connect`, taken out on dispose) when `VisualBasicRuntime.IsProgramAssembly`. Code that names these types in the Host must not run before it is known that the assembly is the program's (the JIT binds the name); `Connect` is a separate `NoInlining` method for that reason. `IScriptUi.ShowError(string)` is new (`AppScriptUi`: message box titled "ComicRack" through `ILibraryDialogs.ShowMessageAsync`).
- Tests: `Compat.VisualBasic.Tests/FileSystemTests` (24: Trash and no-Trash routes, failing Trash and failing dialog, argument errors without dialog, copy/move with conflicts, surface drift against the framework's `FileSystem` and its 74 other types, the Framework's strong name binds to the assembly), Host.Tests `VisualBasicFileSystemTests` (script deletes through the host with a fake Trash, error reaches the script UI, services connected and removed) and `AssemblyIdentityTests`, `identity-probe.py` scenario `visual_basic_file_system` (also in the signed bundle), `make-app.sh` lists the file. `plugin-gap.sh` regenerated: `FileSystem.DeleteFile` is `real` (rule and notes lines removed from `gap-rules.txt`).


**Facts from P4.17.5 (2026-10-28)**
- Decision record `mac/reports/path-separators.md`. Chosen: (a) + (b), links with the odd name made by `ComicRack.Mac.Host.Scripting.PathAliases` (`Sync(root)`, `Adopt(folder)`, `RemoveSiblingLinks(folder)`, `Literals(text)`, `HasAlias(text)`); (c) (facade members accepting a backslash) not done, it cannot cover `File.Exists` or `File.Create`. `PluginPackages.Commit` calls it on install, update and uninstall; `ScriptHost.Start` calls it for the user folder and the extra folders (not the bundled one) before the scripts are read; `crcli script run` does not.
- **Two spellings of one join**: `__file__[:-len('x.py')] + "\F"` keeps the trailing separator and names `D/\F`; `FileInfo(__file__).DirectoryName + "\F"` has none and names `<parent of D>/<D>\F` (a file beside the plugin's folder). Both links are made for every `\F` that a script of `D` names and `D/F` exists (relative targets `F` and `D/F`). A sub path (`\Images\x.png`) links to `Images/x.png`. A name ending in `.py` or `.xml` gets no link (`PluginEngine.Initialize` reads every such file below the folder).
- **A link to a missing file is harmful**: `File.Exists` on a dangling symbolic link is true on .NET 10 (measured), so the plugin would skip `File.Create` and then fail to load the file. Links exist only for files that exist; a file the plugin creates under the odd name (`File.Create(path + "\App.Config")`) is a regular file with that name until the next `Sync`, which moves it to `App.Config` (the newer of two wins) and makes the odd name a link to it. This is also what lets the plugin's own `Save(... + "App.Config")` and `Load(... + "\App.Config")` meet, and what makes `KeepFiles=App.Config` keep the settings through an update (`CommitInstall` adopts before it deletes).
- String constants are found by a text scan of the top-level `*.py` of each folder (Python 2 escapes resolved, raw strings, comments skipped), not by the parser: a start-up cost of a file read, no scan. The scanner finding `windows-path` for `\name.ext` (rule shared through `PathAliases.HasAlias`) is `info` now; drive paths and Windows programs stay warnings and have no owner task any more (rule line replaced by a comment in `mac/scripting/gap-rules.txt`).
- `exit` and `quit` are builtins of every script engine (`ScriptEngineDefaults.Prepare`, as the `site` module defines them; IronPython's hosting API does not import `site`): `Translate()` of the first acceptance plugin names `exit` in an `except` branch once its 152 translations are read. With the links it reads all 152 entries; `LoadSetting()` creates `App.Config` and reads it again.
- Tests: Host.Tests `Scripting/PathAliasTests` (22: literal decoding, links in the folder and beside it, no link for a missing file or `.py`, stale removal, adoption, newer wins, install/update/uninstall with `KeepFiles`, a hand-made folder run by a script, `exit`/`quit`), `Acceptance/PathSeparatorAcceptanceTests` (first plugin: `Translate()` and `LoadSetting()` unmodified, settings read back after a restart, update keeps them, uninstall leaves nothing; second plugin: the 12 `Bitmap.FromFile(SCRIPTDIRECTORY + "\\name")` expressions of its own `configureform.py` load). `plugin-gap.sh` regenerated.

**Facts from P4.9a (2026-10-27)**
- Where it lives: `mac/cYo.Compat.Forms/Model/` (hand-written partial parts, merged with the generated ones by the P4.7 rule): `Control.cs` (state, hierarchy, ambient properties, visibility, focus, handles, dispose), `Control.Events.cs` (the 71 events and their `OnXxx`, produced once from the reference; the ones with propagation are in `Control.cs`), `ControlCollection.cs` (`Control.ControlCollection` and the `ArrangedElementCollection` base), `Form.cs` + `Form.Events.cs`, `Containers.cs` (`ScrollableControl`, `ContainerControl`, `UserControl`, `Panel`, `GroupBox`), `Controls.cs` (`Label`, `ButtonBase`, `Button`, `CheckBox`, `RadioButton`, `TextBoxBase`, `TextBox`), `Lists.cs` (`ListControl`, `ListBox`, `ComboBox` with their `ObjectCollection`s, `UpDownBase`, `NumericUpDown`), `ListControl.Events.cs`, `EventArgs.cs` (13 event data classes), `Backend.cs` (the seam), `AssemblyInfo.cs`. `Application.EnableVisualStyles` and `SetCompatibleTextRenderingDefault` are no-ops now; `Application.ThreadException` is raised (by `FormsRuntime.Guard`). `Control.Handle` stays unsupported (P4.17.6); `Control.cs` at the project root keeps `IWin32Window`, `TreeView`, `TreeNode` and the `Handle` stub.
- **Generator rule (P4.9a, `mac/tools/facade-gen`, Forms set only)**: a generated member that overrides or hides (the `new` properties and events that the reference re-declares to hide them in the designer) a member of a base class calls it (`=> base.X`) when that member is implemented, i.e. declared by a hand-written part or itself generated as a forward; abstract base members, indexers and statics are never forwarded, the type and the accessors must match, and the Drawing and WPF sets are untouched. Result: implementing `Control.Text` once also serves `Label.Text`, `Form.Text`, `TextBox.Text` and every other control, and `Control.Click` serves the 70 `new` events of the subclasses (1720 members forwarded now; the generated header of a file with a forward says so). To implement a member of a subclass differently, declare it by hand there. `FacadeGeneratorTests` has two tests for the rule; `plugin-gap` classifies a forward as `real` (its IL is no longer `call FacadeStub.Member; throw`).
- **The seam is `cYo.Compat.Forms` (namespace), not `System.Windows.Forms`**: `IFormsBackend`, `IControlPeer`, `IFormPeer`, `ModalLoop`, `FormsRuntime`, `NullFormsBackend`, `NullPeer` are the only public types of the facade that the reference lacks (`ShimSurfaceTests` allows exactly these). Everything a peer calls back is `internal` with `InternalsVisibleTo("ComicRack.Mac.FormsBackend")` (the project name P4.10a takes) and `Compat.Forms.Tests`: `Control.PeerClick()`, `Control.Notify(property)`, `TextBoxBase.UserChangedText`, `UpDownBase.UserChangedText/CommitEdit`, `ComboBox.UserSelectedIndex`, `Form.CloseByUser()`, `Control.FocusCore`, `Control.CreateControl(ignoreVisible)`; a public member that the reference lacks on a reference type fails `The_members_are_those_of_the_reference` (the same rule applies to P4.9b to P4.13). `IFormsBackend`: `CheckAccess/Invoke/Post`, `RunModal(ModalLoop)` (a nested loop that returns when `ModalLoop.Exit()` was called, also before it ran), `DoEvents`, `CreatePeer(Control)` (called parent first when a handle is created), `MeasureText`, `GetScreenBounds`, `GetModifierKeys/GetMouseButtons/GetMousePosition`. `IControlPeer`: `PropertyChanged(name)` with the WinForms property name (plus `Controls`, `ZOrder`, `Selection`, `Items`, `Range`), `Invalidate(area)`, `ChildrenChanged()`, `Focus()`; `IFormPeer` adds `Show(owner)`, `ShowModal(owner)`, `Hide`, `Close`, `Activate`. `FormsRuntime.Backend` is the installed backend or a `NullFormsBackend` created on first use. The default null backend has **no thread affinity** (every thread may touch peers, `Post` runs its action at once, so Shown follows Activated inside `Show()` and a script thread can never wait for a UI thread that does not exist: the app runs scripts on pool threads before P4.10a installs a real backend); tests use `new NullFormsBackend { ThreadAffinity = true }` (the creating thread is the UI thread, `Post` queues, `Pump()` runs the queue, `Invoke` from another thread waits for it) and `RunModal` returns at once when the queue is empty unless `ModalExitsWhenIdle = false`; `FormsRuntime.Reset()` for tests; `FormsRuntime.UnhandledException`/`Unhandled` collect what `Application.ThreadException` did not take.
- State and threading: every field group is read and written under the control's lock (bounds, colours, padding are multi-word), events are raised outside the lock on the calling thread, and a peer is told through `Notify`, which posts to the backend when the caller is not on the UI thread (P4.9e adds coalescing, `Invoke`, `InvokeRequired`, `DoEvents`, `Timer`). A peer exists only while the handle is created (a shown form and its controls); the facade state is the truth and `ChildrenChanged` asks the peer to rebuild its children from `Controls`.
- Orders implemented (from the dotnet/winforms source, checked by `ModelTests` and `FormsModelScriptTests`; P4.9g compares them with Windows traces): `Text` raises `TextChanged` only when the value changes; a size change raises Layout (of the control), Resize, SizeChanged, ClientSizeChanged and a location change Move, LocationChanged; `Controls.Add` raises ParentChanged (child, then the changed ambient values), Layout and ControlAdded (owner), `Remove` ParentChanged then ControlRemoved; adding a child again moves it to the back; TabIndex is assigned in order of adding. `Visible` of a child is its flag and all its parents' flags (the flag is its own); EnabledChanged and VisibleChanged go to the children whose own flag is set; BackColor, ForeColor, Font, Cursor, RightToLeft are inherited until set and the children hear of a change. `Form.Show`: HandleCreated (form, then children), Load (while `Visible` is still false), VisibleChanged (form, children), Activated, and Shown posted to the backend queue (first show only; run at once by the default null backend); a second form's Show raises the first one's Deactivate before its own Activated. `Close()` reports `CloseReason.UserClosing` (as the Framework does), raises Closing, FormClosing (both can cancel), Closed, FormClosed, Deactivate, then disposes (HandleDestroyed of the form, of the children, Disposed of the children, Disposed of the form); owned forms close first with `FormOwnerClosing`; a form that never had a handle is only disposed. Focus: `Focus()` raises Enter (outer container first), GotFocus on the new control, then LostFocus, Leave, Validating, Validated on the old one; `Select`, `ActiveControl =` and `SelectNextControl` raise Leave, Validating, Validated, LostFocus; a cancelled Validating keeps the focus; a hidden or disabled control loses it; a shown form focuses its `ActiveControl` or the first selectable control in tab order. `Button.OnClick` sets `Form.DialogResult` before Click; `CheckBox` click toggles before Click and raises CheckedChanged before CheckStateChanged; `RadioButton.Checked` unchecks its container's other buttons first; `ListBox` raises SelectedValueChanged before SelectedIndexChanged; `ComboBox` updates `Text` (TextChanged) before SelectedIndexChanged; `NumericUpDown.Value` raises ValueChanged, then formats `Text`.
- The window frame is approximated: `Form.Size = ClientSize + Control.NonClientSize`, Windows 10 at 96 dpi: Sizable 16 x 39 (the 284 x 261 client area of a 300 x 300 form), FixedSingle and FixedDialog 8 x 31, Fixed3D 12 x 35, tool windows 8 x 26 and 16 x 34, None 0 x 0; changing `FormBorderStyle` keeps the client size. The values other than Sizable are derived from the Windows metrics, not measured: P4.9g's Windows recorder confirms them. A panel, label, text box, list box or up-down with a border has 2 (single) or 4 (3D) pixels around the client area.
- Not in this task (state exists, behaviour comes later): layout of children (Anchor, Dock, Margin, Padding, AutoSize, Suspend/ResumeLayout counters and the Layout event exist; `Control.LayoutChildren` is the hook P4.9b fills), `GetPreferredSize` (Size.Empty), fonts (`Font`/`SystemFonts` still throw in Drawing, so `Control.DefaultFont` is null; ambient fonts are tested by P4.9d), `Invoke`/`BeginInvoke`/`InvokeRequired`/`Timer`/`DoEvents` (P4.9e), `ShowDialog`, `DialogResult` effects, `AcceptButton`/`CancelButton` keys, `MessageBox` (P4.9f; `DialogResult`, `AcceptButton`, `CancelButton`, `Owner` already hold their values), drawing and `Paint` (P4.10e; `Invalidate` raises Invalidated and tells the peer), mouse and keyboard entry points (`OnMouseDown`... work when called; the peers' routing, with the Click, MouseClick, MouseUp order of WinForms, is P4.10a/b), clipboard methods of text boxes, `CheckedListBox`, `ListView`, `TreeView`, `TabControl` and the other controls (P4.10b to P4.10g).
- `mac/scripting/plugin-gap.sh` regenerated `mac/reports/plugin-gap-bedetheque.md` (P4.18 records deltas from here): Bedetheque Scraper 2 6.08 has 280 real and 29 stub rows (was 194 and 110), Library Organizer 2.1.13 has 356 real and 101 stub rows (was 211 and 248); the remaining stubs belong to P4.9b to P4.9f, P4.10a to P4.10g and P4.17.x.
- Tests: `Compat.Forms.Tests/ModelTests` (59 tests: state, hierarchy, ambient values, show and close traces, peers and threads, focus orders, buttons, text box, list box, combo box, numeric up-down, backend), `FacadeGeneratorTests` (+2), `ShimSurfaceTests` (+1, the seam namespace), `GeneratedStubTests` (rewritten around members that still throw), `ComicRack.Mac.Host.Tests/Scripting/FormsModelScriptTests` (3: a script builds a dialog, derives from Form, Control and UserControl, and the trace is compared as a list) and `GeneratedSurfaceScriptTests`.

**Facts from P4.9b (2026-10-28)**
- Where it lives: `mac/cYo.Compat.Forms/Model/Layout.cs` (MIT header of dotnet/winforms kept; a reduced port of `DefaultLayout`, written against the facade's `Control`, not copied): `LayoutEngine` (now hand-written and complete: `InitLayout` no-op, `Layout` false), `DefaultLayoutEngine` (internal singleton behind `Control.LayoutEngine`), `AnchorInfo`, `DefaultLayout` (`InitLayout`, `UpdateAnchorInfo`, `Layout`). `Control.LayoutEngine` is public virtual: **P4.9c overrides it** in `FlowLayoutPanel`/`TableLayoutPanel` with an engine of its own; `Control.OnLayout` raises the Layout event, then calls `LayoutEngine.Layout(this, e)` (the internal `LayoutChildren` hook of P4.9a is gone). `ScrollableControl.DisplayRectangle` (client area, at least `AutoScrollMinSize` with AutoScroll, minus Padding) and `ScrollableControl.DockPadding` (`DockPaddingEdges` over `Padding`) are hand-written; `GroupBox.DisplayRectangle` was already there.
- Algorithm: the children are arranged in three passes over a copy of their bounds (`rects[]`, the cached bounds of the original) and only the changed ones are applied, in the order of `Controls`, with `Control.SetBoundsByLayout` (= `SetBoundsCore(..., BoundsSpecified.None)`). (1) Docked controls, in reverse z-order (the child added last docks first; `SendToBack` makes a control dock first, `BringToFront` last) within the display rectangle: Top/Bottom take the width of what is left and the control's own height (an AutoSize control asks `GetPreferredSize(new Size(width, 1))`), Left/Right the height of what is left and the control's width; Fill takes what is left; min/max size are applied before the rest shrinks; Margin is ignored; only controls whose own Visible flag is set take part (`Control.VisibleFlag`). (2) Anchored controls (`Dock == None` and `Anchor != TopLeft`): `AnchorInfo` holds the distances to the display rectangle (left/top from its origin; right/bottom from the far edge when anchored there, from the middle when anchored on neither side), the destination is computed from it and the display rectangle, a negative size becomes 0 and the distances survive (the stretched control returns to its size). (3) AutoSize and not docked: the control grows to `GetPreferredSize(hint)` (GrowOnly) or takes it (GrowAndShrink) towards the sides it is not anchored to (anchored right only: leftwards, bottom only: upwards). `GetPreferredSize` returns `Size.Empty` until P4.9d for controls that cannot measure themselves; an empty answer means "no opinion" for the engine (the control keeps its size), so P4.9d needs no change here. The base `Control` default `AutoSizeMode` is GrowOnly (it was GrowAndShrink by an enum default before).
- When a control learns its distances: `AnchorInfo` is taken from the present bounds and the container's present `DisplayRectangle` when the control is added (`Control.InitLayout`), when its Anchor is set, and when a script sets its bounds (any `BoundsSpecified` other than None); the engine's own placement never changes it. As in the original, controls that are added while the container still has its default size (200 x 100 for a `Panel`, 284 x 261 client for a `Form`) take their distances from that size: a script that anchors must size the container first (designer order). Dock is exclusive with Anchor: setting Dock to anything but None makes Anchor read TopLeft, setting Anchor on a docked control first sets Dock to None (the old bounds come back); `Dock = None` restores the **specified bounds**: `Control.specifiedBounds` takes x, y, width and height only when that part was specified by the caller (`Left`, `Width`, `Bounds`, `SetBounds(...)` set them, the engine does not).
- Layout triggers (property name of `LayoutEventArgs.AffectedProperty`): "Bounds" (the parent, after any change of location or size of a child, after `InitLayout` has seen the new bounds: `SetBoundsCore` suspends the parent's layout around the change and resumes with `true`; and the control itself from `OnResize`), "Parent" (Controls.Add/Remove), "ChildIndex", "Dock", "Anchor", "Visible" (when the **own** visible flag of a child changes), "MinimumSize"/"MaximumSize", "AutoSize", "Margin" (parent), "Padding" (self). `PerformLayout` keeps the control suspended while `OnLayout` runs (the original's `layoutSuspendCount = 1`): a child that changes inside the engine or inside a Layout handler only marks the layout pending and the mark is dropped at the end, so no nested layout and no second Layout event; `SuspendLayout` counts are guarded by the control's lock now; `ResumeLayout(false)` keeps the pending mark. `MinimumSize`/`MaximumSize` apply in `SetBoundsCore` for every `specified` value (also None).
- Not in this task: preferred size of containers (an AutoSize `Panel`/`Form`/`GroupBox` does not grow to its children; the measure-only path of `DefaultLayout` is not ported), AutoScroll extents and scroll bars (the display rectangle only knows `AutoScrollMinSize`; `AutoScrollPosition` offsets are not applied), `RightToLeft` mirroring of Left/Right, the DPI quirk for anchors that lie outside the container, `TabPage`/`SplitContainer`/`ToolStripContainer` specifics. Whether a location-only change of a docked child makes the parent lay out again (the control then snaps back) is kept from P4.9a (the original may not do it); P4.9g's Windows recorder decides.
- Tests: `Compat.Forms.Tests/LayoutTests` (34: rectangles worked out by hand from the algorithm for the five dock styles and their z-order, padding and `DockPadding`, Margin, hidden controls, `Dock = None` restore, Dock/Anchor exclusion, min/max, nested panels, a form's client area, every anchor combination, the middle, the distances at add time and when a script moves a control, recovery from a negative width, suspended and resumed layout, the Layout events a placement raises, AutoSize growth with a test control that measures, the engine object, AutoScroll, GroupBox) and `ComicRack.Mac.Host.Tests/Scripting/FormsModelScriptTests` (+1: a script lays out a dialog with Dock and Anchor and resizes it). `facade-gen` regenerated (`LayoutEngine.g.cs` gone, forwards of `LayoutEngine` overrides in the generated subclasses).

**Facts from P4.9c (2026-10-28)**
- Where it lives: `mac/cYo.Compat.Forms/Model/FlowLayout.cs` (`LayoutSettings` base, `FlowLayoutSettings`, `FlowLayoutPanel`, the engine `FlowLayout`, and `CellLayout`, the helpers both engines share: unified anchor of a control, `Align` along one axis, `ElementSize`, `Participants`, `Constrain`) and `Model/TableLayout.cs` (`TableLayoutStyle`, `ColumnStyle`, `RowStyle`, the three style collections, `TableLayoutPanelCellPosition`, `TableLayoutCellPaintEventArgs`, `TableLayoutSettings`, `TableLayoutControlCollection`, `TableLayoutPanel`, the engine `TableLayout`). Written against the facade's `Control` (MIT header kept; not copied from dotnet/winforms). `facade-gen` regenerated: the generated parts of these types are gone or reduced to what is still unimplemented (`TableLayoutPanel.ScaleControl/ScaleCore` for P4.9d, `TableLayoutSettings` serialization).
- **Engine seam**: `LayoutEngine.TryGetPreferredSize(Control, Size, out Size)` is a new `internal virtual` (false = "no opinion", the Dock/Anchor engine); `Control.GetPreferredSize` asks the layout engine and adds `Padding.Size + NonClientSize`, a proposed side of 0 means "no limit" (in the original it becomes `int.MaxValue`). The container answers correctly for AutoSize use, but **nothing resizes an AutoSize `FlowLayoutPanel`/`TableLayoutPanel` to it yet**: their `Layout` only returns `AutoSize` (P4.9d). `Control.SpecifiedSize` (internal) is the size the script gave: both engines size a control that is not AutoSize (or cannot measure itself) by it, never by its present size, so a stretched or docked control does not feed its stretched size back into a column, a row or a flow cell; AutoSize controls are asked `GetPreferredSize(Size.Empty)` (flow) or `(cell width, 0)` (table rows).
- **FlowLayout**: a cell is the control plus its Margin; a row ends when the next cell would end behind the display rectangle (a row that is exactly full does not wrap) or after a control with a flow break (only with WrapContents); the first control of a row never wraps; the row is as high as its highest cell, and without wrapping as high as the display rectangle (at least the highest cell), so Dock/Anchor have an extent to stretch in. Along the flow the control keeps its size; across it the unified anchor decides (Top and Bottom stretch, one of them aligns, none centers; Dock Top/Bottom/Left/Right/Fill stand for Top+Left+Right, Bottom+Left+Right, Left+Top+Bottom, Right+Top+Bottom, all four). Directions: LeftToRight rows go down; RightToLeft starts at the right edge (leading margin = Right) and rows go down; TopDown columns go right; BottomUp starts at the bottom edge and columns go right. Flow breaks and cell numbers are kept in a `ConditionalWeakTable` per control (the attached properties of WinForms), so they survive a move to another panel. `FlowLayoutPanel.Settings` (internal) is the `FlowLayoutSettings` object.
- **TableLayout**: cells are assigned in two passes: children with a column and a row first, then the others in the order of `Controls` with a monotonic cursor (row by row for AddRows and FixedSize with `max(ColumnCount, 1)` columns, column by column for AddColumns with `max(RowCount, 1)` rows; a span that does not fit the rest of the line starts the next one; only a column or only a row given: the first free cell of it). Hidden controls (own Visible flag) take no cell. FixedSize throws `ArgumentException` from the layout when a control does not fit (so from `Controls.Add`). The table grows by what the assignment found and **`ColumnCount`/`RowCount` follow the growth** (`TableLayoutSettings.GrowTo`, never shrinking). Sizes: Absolute = `(int)value`; AutoSize = largest single-span control (preferred or specified size plus Margin); spans take the narrowest first and make the AutoSize columns they cover wider by the missing amount (equal share, the rest to the last one; Absolute and Percent columns are never changed by a span); Percent shares `available - (everything else + lines)` by `(int)(remaining * value / totalPercent)`, the last percent column takes the rest; measuring (`GetPreferredSize`) counts a Percent column by its content. A control is placed in its cell minus Margin by its unified anchor and cut to the cell (`Min(size, cell)`) when it does not stretch. `CellBorderStyle` lines: width 1 (Single), 2 (Inset, Outset), 3 (the double styles and OutsetPartial), before, between and after the cells, taken from the cell sizes (an Absolute column of 100 is 100 pixels of cell plus its line). `GetColumnWidths/GetRowHeights/GetPositionFromControl/GetControlFromPosition` compute the plan on demand (not cached). `TableLayoutPanel.LayoutSettings` setter accepts only the object the panel gave out. `CellPaint` has its event and `OnCellPaint`; nothing raises it (drawing: P4.10e).
- Layout trigger names (`LayoutEventArgs.AffectedProperty`): "FlowDirection", "WrapContents", "FlowBreak" (the parent, with the child), "ColumnCount", "RowCount", "GrowStyle", "CellBorderStyle", "Style" (any change of a style or of its collection), "TableIndex" (SetColumn, SetRow, SetCellPosition), "ColumnSpan", "RowSpan". `TableLayoutControlCollection.Add(control, column, row)` sets the cell before it adds the control, so the table is laid out once.
- **From memory of the dotnet/winforms source, not from a Windows run** (P4.9g's recorder settles them): the row height of a non-wrapping flow; that `ColumnCount`/`RowCount` read the grown values; `(int)` truncation for Absolute sizes and the Percent shares with the remainder in the last percent column; narrowest-span-first with the equal share for spans over AutoSize columns; percent columns having no minimum of their own (the original may keep the `MinimumSize` of their controls); `Min(size, cell)` clipping; the FixedSize exception being thrown by the layout and not by `Controls.Add` itself; RightToLeft mirroring of the controls' own `RightToLeft` property is not ported (FlowDirection.RightToLeft is).
- Tests: `Compat.Forms.Tests/FlowLayoutTests` (32: rectangles worked out by hand for wrap, the exactly full row, flow breaks, the four directions, cross axis anchors and docks, margins, padding, hidden controls, min/max, AutoSize elements, Layout triggers, preferred size) and `TableLayoutTests` (45: styles Absolute, AutoSize and Percent and their mixture, rounding, spans and their growth of AutoSize columns, auto placement for the three grow styles, explicit cells, growth of the counts, FixedSize, anchors and docks in a cell, margin, padding, cell borders, the cell properties, the style collections, preferred size, Layout triggers, nested tables), `ComicRack.Mac.Host.Tests/Scripting/FormsModelScriptTests` (+1: a script lays out a FlowLayoutPanel and a TableLayoutPanel and changes them). `mac/scripting/plugin-gap.sh` regenerated `mac/reports/plugin-gap-bedetheque.md`: Library Organizer 2.1.13 361 real and 97 stub rows (was 357 and 101); `FlowLayoutPanel` is no longer a stub for either acceptance plugin.

**Facts from P4.9e (2026-10-28)**
- Where it lives: `mac/cYo.Compat.Forms/Model/Control.Threading.cs` (what a peer is told, `Invoke`, `BeginInvoke`, `EndInvoke`, `InvokeRequired`, the internal `MarshaledCall`), `Model/Timer.cs`, `Application.DoEvents` in `Application.cs`, `ITimerPeer` and `IFormsBackend.CreateTimer` in `Model/Backend.cs`. `facade-gen` regenerated: `Timer.g.cs` is gone; the four `Control.InvokeAsync` overloads (.NET 9, not in the Framework scripts are written for) stay generated stubs.
- **What a peer is told** (`Control.Notify`, `NotifyPeerChildren`, `NotifyPeerInvalidate`): on the UI thread the peer is called at once; from another thread the call is posted to the backend and **coalesced**: one pending post per control and property name (a set under a leaf lock, removed when the post runs, so a later change posts again), one for `ChildrenChanged`, and one for `Invalidate` whose area is the union of the areas that were posted meanwhile. The post goes to the peer that exists when it runs (dropped when the handle was destroyed, delivered to the new peer after `RecreateHandle`); the peer reads the facade state, so it sees the last value. 4 threads x 1,000 `Text` writes leave one pending post (test). Peers are called only on the UI thread; a peer for a child added from a worker is created by `backend.Invoke` (the worker waits for the UI thread to pump).
- **Thread-safe state**: the multi-word and compound values that were not yet under a lock now are (lock = the control's own, `Control.SyncRoot`): `NumericUpDown` `Value`, `Minimum`, `Maximum`, `Increment` (decimals; the range check and the assignment are one step, events run outside the lock) and its edit text (`UpDownBase.SetFormattedText` is the program's own change of the field and does not go through the `ChangingText` flag, so a racing reader cannot take it for a user edit and parse a stale text back into `Value`; the text loops until it shows the last value), `Form.TransparencyKey`, `MaximizedBounds`, `RestoreBounds`, `ScrollableControl.AutoScrollMargin/MinSize/Position`, `ContainerControl.AutoScaleDimensions`, `Control.AutoScrollOffset`. Already safe from P4.9a/b: bounds, colours, padding, margin, min/max size, text, the control and item collections, the layout counters. Not promised: two properties read together (Size and Location), and the layout result while writers race (after one `PerformLayout` on one thread it is that of a single-threaded run; test). An event of a setter (`TextChanged`, `Resize` ...) runs on the thread that wrote the value (section 9).
- **Invoke family** (follows `Control.MarshaledInvoke` of dotnet/winforms): the marshaler is the control or its nearest parent with a handle; `InvokeRequired` is false when there is none and otherwise `!backend.CheckAccess()`; `Invoke`/`BeginInvoke` without any handle throw `InvalidOperationException("Invoke or BeginInvoke cannot be called on a control until the window handle has been created.")`. Calls go into the marshaler's FIFO and are run by a post (`marshaler.InvokeMarshaledCallbacks`); a synchronous call on the UI thread runs at once **after the calls queued before it**; `EndInvoke` on the UI thread runs the queue when the call is still pending, elsewhere it waits; `EndInvoke` of another control's result or a foreign `IAsyncResult` throws `ArgumentException`. The delegate runs in the `ExecutionContext` of the caller; `EventHandler` (sender = the control, `EventArgs.Empty`, or the first two arguments), `MethodInvoker` and `WaitCallback` are called directly, any other delegate with `DynamicInvoke`; an exception comes back as its `GetBaseException()` (also from `Invoke`), is rethrown by `Invoke`/`EndInvoke`, and an exception of a `BeginInvoke` call goes to `Application.ThreadException` (or `FormsRuntime.Unhandled`) and stays for `EndInvoke`. When the control is disposed (`DestroyHandle`, not during `RecreateHandle`) the calls that did not run complete with `ObjectDisposedException` (a worker blocked in `Invoke` is released); a call is refused or cancelled, never lost (the handle is tested under the queue's lock). The overloads `Invoke(Action)`, `Invoke<T>(Func<T>)`, `BeginInvoke(Action)` exist. On a backend without a UI thread (the default `NullFormsBackend`) `CheckAccess()` is always true: `Invoke` and `BeginInvoke` run at once on the calling thread and `InvokeRequired` is false, so a headless script never waits.
- **Timer** (`Model/Timer.cs`): `Interval` 100 by default, below 1 throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` ("Value of '0' is not valid for 'Interval'. 'Interval' must be greater than 0."); `Enabled`/`Start`/`Stop` are state under a lock and tell the backend through the posts of the thread order; starting a running timer does nothing, a new `Interval` while running starts the interval again, `Dispose` stops. Each start (and interval change) is a `TimerRun` with its own toolkit timer from `IFormsBackend.CreateTimer(Action tick)`; the tick of an ended run is dropped, so a tick that was queued before `Stop` is never delivered, also not after `Start` again. Ticks come on the UI thread (the Framework ticks on the thread that enabled the timer, through its message loop), a late timer ticks once (WM_TIMER rule: the backend must not queue one tick per missed interval), a handler exception goes to `Application.ThreadException`. `NullFormsBackend` has a **manual clock**: `AdvanceTime(ms)` ticks the running timers in order of due time (equal times: oldest timer first) through `Post` and returns the ticks queued or run; there is no real clock, so a headless process has no ticks.
- **`IFormsBackend` additions for P4.10a**: `CreateTimer(Action tick)` returning `ITimerPeer` (`Start(interval)`, `Stop()`, `Dispose()`, all called on the UI thread; `Start` on a running timer restarts it). `Post` must run actions in FIFO order and `RunModal` must serve posts while it waits (the Invoke tests run the UI thread inside a nested frame, and a call opened inside another call). `DoEvents` stays as written in P4.9a (runs what is queued; off the UI thread nothing). `NullFormsBackend` got `AdvanceTime`, `Clock`, `RunningTimers`, `PendingCount`; `NullPeer` got `LastInvalidated`. The seam test lists `ITimerPeer`.
- `Application.DoEvents()` calls `FormsRuntime.Backend.DoEvents()`; `Application.Run`, `Exit`, `ExitThread`, `OpenForms`, `MessageLoop` stay stubs (P4.9f, the gap rules say so).
- Tests: `Compat.Forms.Tests/ThreadingTests` (34: text, rectangle, colour, padding, margin, size, point, decimal and form values hammered by 4 threads x 1,000 writes with a concurrent reader, no torn value, no lost change event, items and children added by 4 threads, docked children laid out afterwards, one post per property and one union for `Invalidate`, a post for a destroyed peer, peers of controls added by workers, `InvokeRequired`, `Invoke` and `BeginInvoke` order and exceptions, 4 workers x 250 `Invoke` calls in a nested frame, a call that opens a nested frame while other workers still call, a blocked worker released by `Close`, `DoEvents`, and eleven timer cases on the manual clock; no timing assertion, every wait has a 60 s deadline that only bounds a hang; 40 sequential and 24 parallel runs were clean) and `ComicRack.Mac.Host.Tests/Scripting/FormsModelScriptTests` (+1: an IronPython script on a backend with thread affinity updates a label from a .NET thread, calls `Invoke`, `BeginInvoke`/`EndInvoke`, loops on `Application.DoEvents()` and runs a `Timer` with `AdvanceTime`). No HiDPI test: nothing here draws.
- `mac/scripting/plugin-gap.sh` regenerated `mac/reports/plugin-gap-bedetheque.md`: Bedetheque Scraper 2 6.08 has 288 real and 22 stub rows (was 280 and 29), Library Organizer 2.1.13 has 363 real and 96 stub rows (was 361 and 97); the rules for `Invoke`, `BeginInvoke`, `EndInvoke`, `InvokeRequired`, `DoEvents` and `Timer` are removed from `mac/scripting/gap-rules.txt`, the rule for the rest of `Application` names P4.9f.

## 4. Checkpoints (the only times the owner is asked)

A checkpoint is a block in `mac/plans/OWNER-ACTIONS.md` (template in `RUNBOOK.md` §6). The orchestrator writes it when the checkpoint's trigger is reached and keeps working on everything that does not depend on the answer. It STOPs only when no independent task is left.

| # | When it occurs | What the owner is asked | Why then | If there is no answer |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **C1** Samples and corpus | Now (written in the initial inbox; optional, non-blocking) | (a) one or two real `.cbr` files and a `.cb7` made by real 7-Zip, copied to `mac/fixtures/owner/` (git-ignored); (b) the plugins you use (names, or a copy of your Scripts folder to `mac/fixtures/owner/scripts/`); (c) optional: a copy of your Windows `Config.xml` to `mac/fixtures/owner/Config.xml`; (d) optional: your notes from `mac/plans/mvp-acceptance.md` §8.12; (e) optional (added by P5.T3, R9): screenshots of the Windows app for the views it lists, to `mac/fixtures/owner/reference/` | P0.7b, P2.4b, P4.6b and P5.28b use them; (e) becomes the reference column of the contact sheets; the earlier the better | CBR/real-7z tests stay skipped (recorded as unverified); the corpus is the bundled samples plus public plugins; `Config.xml` import is tested on a synthetic file |
| **C2** Mid-port review | After wave B (§6): about 35 tasks in, when the main window has editing, context menus and write-back | 30 to 60 minutes with a copy of your library: (a) the checks only a person can do: trackpad swipe and pinch feel, scroll feel, Retina sharpness, Metal frame rate; (b) an SMB share scan and the TCC prompts; (c) a CBR from your library; (d) push `macos` to `origin` once and look at the Actions tab (P3.9 CI; if P5.T5 exists, run "mac-reference-shots" once and unzip its artifact into `mac/fixtures/owner/reference/ci/`); (e) approve or reject pending edit proposals and upstream proposals, if any; (f) **chrome review, about 10 minutes**: open `mac/artifacts/chrome/C2/sheet.png` (or `sheet.html`), one row per view: reference, Mac light, Mac dark; answer per row "ok" or what differs, and confirm or flip the chrome-guidelines §7 defaults that have a flip cost (C-MENU-3, C-TB-1, C-TREE-1, C-DLG-2 button order, C-PREF-1, C-CTRL-1, C-FONT-1 small captions) | First moment the app is used like a daily tool; real-input defaults (swipe threshold, pinch speed) can still be tuned cheaply; the chrome foundation is done but most screens are not built yet, so a flip is cheap | Work continues through waves C to F; the items move to C6; the §7 defaults stand |
| **C3** Script layer go/no-go (conditional) | Only if spike P4.1 (IronPython) or P4.2 (modal loop, identity mapping) ends NO-GO or needs a choice that weakens D6 | Choose between the options the spike wrote (for example IronPython 3.4 with a compatibility notice, or modeless-only dialogs) | D6 ("scripts run unmodified") is binding; only the owner can relax it | All non-script work continues; scripts run headless only (P4.3 to P4.6); the Script UI tasks (P4.7 onwards) stay blocked |
| **C4** Plugin acceptance | After P4.15 (end of wave E) | Run your top plugins on the Mac with `mac/plans/plugin-acceptance.md` (P4.15), about 1 hour | Only you know what "works" means for your scripts | v1 ships with the automated corpus results; the checklist moves to C6 |
| **C5** Release identity and credentials | When P6.1 to P6.6 are done (or earlier, whenever the owner is ready) | (a) Developer ID Application certificate in the login keychain; (b) `xcrun notarytool store-credentials comicrack-notary ...`; (c) confirm the bundle id `org.comicrackce.ComicRack` and the name `ComicRack CE`, or give others; (d) optional: a 1024 px icon source; (e) GitHub secrets for `mac-release.yml` if you want CI releases | Signing and notarization cannot be done without them; ad-hoc builds continue until then | P6.7 (signed, notarized release candidate) stays blocked; everything else proceeds |
| **C6** v1 acceptance and release go | After P7.8 | `mac/plans/v1-acceptance.md` (all leftover owner-only checks from C2 and C4, a clean-user Gatekeeper launch, the **parity summary**: every ledger deviation with its reason and the `deferred (D4)` list; the **final chrome contact sheet** `mac/artifacts/chrome/C6/sheet.png` of every view, light and dark, about 15 minutes; a final verdict) | End of the plan | Nothing is released; the app stays a local build |

**Checks that remain owner-only** (everything else is automated with `mac/tools/e2e.sh` or headless tests): trackpad gestures (two-finger swipe, pinch, momentum; macOS has no public API to synthesize them), scroll and page-turn *feel*, Metal/ProMotion frame-rate perception and multi-display behaviour, SMB/NAS libraries, TCC prompts on really protected folders, real CBR and real 7-Zip archives (samples), the owner's own plugins, Developer ID signing and notarization, Gatekeeper on a clean user or Mac, VoiceOver judgement, look and feel verdicts in light and dark mode (collected on one contact sheet at C2 and one at C6, chrome-guidelines §9), changes of the system default apps (P5.34).

## 5. Harness design notes (implemented by P0.4, P0.5a to c, P0.6a/b, P0.8)

### 5.1 Replacement manifest and drift guard (P0.4)
- File `mac/replacements.json`:
  ```json
  { "version": 1, "entries": [
    { "original": "ComicRack.Engine/CacheManager.cs",
      "replacement": "mac/ComicRack.Engine.Core/Replacements/PassB/CacheManager.cs",
      "kind": "patched-copy", "pass": "B", "base": "7fd9fd35a9cf829fffc6aa9cd0b44c7325df0cb7",
      "sha256": "ceeb0ad49cf94086f68c5a611996c9f240a5eb500130a1fbbbdcf58a053014e8",
      "maxChangedLines": 2, "reason": "MacImagePool instead of ImagePool (M1.5a)" } ] }
  ```
  `kind` is `excluded`, `stub`, `patched-copy` or `rewrite` (§2.3); `pattern` replaces `original` for glob exclusions (`ComicRack.Engine/IO/Network/**`, hashed as the sorted list of `path sha256` lines of the matched files). Hash = SHA-256 of the file without BOM and with LF line endings (the existing `OriginalSourceDriftTests` rule).
- Tests `mac/tests/ComicRack.Mac.Host.Tests/ReplacementManifestTests.cs`: (1) every original still has its hash (the message names the `mac/tools/replacement-sync.sh` command to run); (2) every `Exclude=`/`Compile Remove=` in `mac/*/Sources.props` and `mac/ComicRack.Plugins.Core/ComicRack.Plugins.Core.csproj` and every file under `mac/*/Replacements/**` has an entry, and every entry points at something that exists; (3) a patched copy differs from its original by at most `maxChangedLines` lines (header comment excluded).
- Tool `mac/tools/replacement-sync.sh <original> [--accept]`: `patched-copy`: writes `git show <base>:<original>`, runs `git merge-file -p <replacement> <base-copy> <original>` into a temp file, shows the result and conflicts, and with `--accept` writes it, then records the new `base` (the upstream commit, `git rev-parse upstream/master`) and `sha256`. `rewrite`/`stub`/`excluded`: prints `git diff <base> upstream/master -- <original>` for a session to port by hand, and `--accept` records the new hash after the port.

### 5.2 Upstream sync harness (P0.5a to c)
- Entry point `mac/tools/upstream-sync-check.sh [--range A..B] [--no-merge] [--record]`. State file `mac/upstream-sync.json` (`lastSyncedUpstream`, date, report digest). Range default: `lastSyncedUpstream..upstream/master`; just after an owner merge it uses the merge commit's second parent.
- Steps and their verdicts (each PASS, WARN, FAIL or INFO, in this order):
  1. Preconditions: on `macos`, no merge in progress (`.git/MERGE_HEAD`), tree clean except `mac/artifacts`, disk guard. Else exit 3.
  2. **Changed and new upstream files** (`git diff --name-status <range>`), classified by `mac/tools/upstream-scan` (P0.5a): *linked* (in the pass B compile items of the three shadow projects, from `dotnet msbuild -getItem:Compile`), *replaced* (manifest), *excluded*, *UI not linked* (`ComicRack/`, `cYo.Common.Windows/`, `cYo.Common.Presentation/` outside the linked files, `ComicRack.Engine.Display.Forms/`, `ComicRack.Engine/Controls/`, `ComicRack.Plugins/Controls/`), *project files* (`*.csproj`: PackageReference and Reference changes compared with `mac/*/Sources.props`), *payload* (`ComicRack/Output/**`: Languages, Scripts, Help, Resources), *build/CI*, *other*. New files that the shadow globs pick up automatically are listed with "compiles: yes/no" from step 4.
  3. **Replacement drift**: the P0.4 manifest test; for each changed original the `replacement-sync.sh` dry run (patched copies: clean auto-merge or conflict; rewrites: diff size).
  4. **Build and tests**: `mac/tools/verify.sh --full` (includes the drift tests).
  5. **Census**: `dotnet run --project mac/tools/census -- --pass A` and `--pass B`, totals must stay 0.
  6. **New platform API** (P0.5b): `api-inventory` against the committed baseline `mac/reports/api-baseline.json`: new `System.Windows.Forms` / `System.Drawing.Common` members with "covered by the shim" yes/no (drawing-coverage logic, Forms surface), new `DllImport`s (module, entry point, declaring type, linked or not). The pass B compile stays the authority for inlined enum constants (`mvp-track.md` §9, M1.2a).
  7. **Summary** to stdout and `mac/artifacts/upstream-sync/report.md`: one line per verdict, the exact follow-up for each FAIL/WARN, and the line `NEXT: ...`.
- Exit codes: 0 clean (with `--record` the state file is updated and committed by the caller), 1 action items a session can fix, 2 build or tests broken, 3 precondition failed.
- The weekly CI dry run (P3.10) runs the same script on a temporary merge in a GitHub runner.

### 5.3 UI automation and e2e (P0.6a/b)
- `mac/tools/ui-drive/ui-drive.sh <command>` plus a Swift helper `mac/tools/ui-drive/WindowTool.swift`, compiled on first use with `swiftc -O` to `mac/artifacts/ui-drive/windowtool` (window list with ids, titles and bounds via `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo`; mouse clicks via `CGEvent`; screen-lock detection via `CGSessionCopyCurrentDictionary`).
- Commands: `launch [--bundle|--dev] [--data-dir D] [-- app args]` (starts `Contents/MacOS/ComicRack.Mac` or the dev build directly so the pid and exit code are known; writes `mac/artifacts/ui-drive/session.env`), `wait-window <title-regex> [s]`, `windows`, `front`, `menu <Menu> <Item> [<Sub>]`, `menu-items <Menu>` (names, shortcuts and check marks through System Events), `key cmd+shift+g`, `keycode <n>`, `type <text>`, `click <x> <y>` (window-relative), `shot <name>` (`screencapture -l<windowid> -o -x` to `mac/artifacts/ui-drive/shots/`), `probe <png> <x> <y>` (pixel colour through `crcli image probe`, added by P0.6a), `choose-path <path>` (in an open or save panel: Cmd+Shift+G, type, Return, Return), `expect-log <regex> [s]` (the app log under the data dir), `quit` (Cmd+Q, waits, prints the exit code), `kill`.
- Safety (§2.10): every input command checks that the frontmost process is the launched pid (`unix id of first process whose frontmost is true`), re-activates once, else aborts with exit 5. Exit 4 = screen locked or no GUI access.
- `mac/tools/e2e.sh [--bundle] [case ...]` runs `mac/tests/e2e/cases/*.sh` (each case sources `mac/tests/e2e/lib.sh`, prints `PASS`/`FAIL`/`SKIP` lines and leaves screenshots); summary and exit 0, 1, or 4 when every case was skipped for a locked screen. Every UI task from P0.6b on adds at least one case.

### 5.4 Unattended loop (P0.8)
- `mac/tools/run-plan.sh [--max-tasks N] [--dry-run]` starts one headless orchestrator run per task (`claude -p "<resume prompt>" --model sonnet --effort high`), checks the `Run state:` line of this file after each run, and ends on `STOPPED`, on a checkpoint STOP, on `--max-tasks`, or after two runs in a row that commit nothing. It needs a permission allowlist (`RUNBOOK.md` §7); `--dangerously-skip-permissions` is the owner's choice and is not used by default.

### 5.5 Parity tooling (P5.T1 to P5.T4)
- `Cr.*` theme tokens with a test that re-reads the original colour tables (P5.T1); `--theme light|dark` app option.
- `Icons.Get(name)` over the original images linked at build time, `Assets/icon-map.json` generated from the `Resources.resx` files with a staleness test (P5.T2).
- `mac/tools/designer-dump/designer-dump.py <Form.Designer.cs>` (view-spec skeleton), `mac/tools/ledger-check/ledger-check.py [--report] [--menus <ui-drive dump>]` (ledger format, coverage of every original menu and toolbar item, comparison of the running app's menus and shortcuts with the ledger), hidden app options `--show <ViewName>` and `--fixture-library <dir>`, `crcli image sheet`, `mac/tools/chrome-sheet.sh <label> [--all|View...]` (contact sheets in `mac/artifacts/chrome/<label>/`) (P5.T3).
- Control themes and custom controls (`CrToolBar`, `CrSplitButton`, `CrTabBar`, `CrStatusBar`, `CrCollapsibleGroup`, `CrSizableContainer`, `CrHeader`, `CrDialogWindow`, `MacAlert`), the chrome gallery (`--show ChromeGallery`) and a lint test against literal colours in views (P5.T4).
- After an upstream merge, `ledger-check` fails on a new or renamed original menu item and the token test fails on a changed original colour: both become U-tasks (`RUNBOOK.md` §5).
